  1 only | *2 only | Both 1 and 2 | Neither 1 nor 2
  x: India is a Union not formed by an agreement of States, and no State can secede; it is an indestructible Union of destructible States.

=== NOTES 2:6 | Union and Its Territory – Articles 1-4, New States and Cession
## SUB 0 | Articles 1–4 – Union, Territory and the First Schedule
[6:0:0] # What each Article says
[6:0:1] TABLE head: Article | Subject
  [6:0:1.0] 1 | "India, that is Bharat, shall be a Union of States"; States and territories in the First Schedule; Art. 1(3) territory of India
  [6:0:1.1] 2 | Admission or establishment of new States (not part of the Union of India)
  [6:0:1.2] 3 | Formation of new States; alteration of areas, boundaries or names of existing States
  [6:0:1.3] 4 | Laws under Arts. 2 and 3 amend the First and Fourth Schedules; Art. 4(2): not deemed an amendment under Art. 368
[6:0:2] - Part I = **Articles 1–4**; the Constitution calls the Centre the **"Union"** (Union Government, Union List), not "Centre".
[6:0:3] - **"Union" not "Federation" (Ambedkar):** the Indian federation is **not the result of an agreement among States**, and **no State has the right to secede** – "an **indestructible Union of destructible States**".
[6:0:4] # Union of India vs Territory of India
[6:0:5] TABLE head: Union of India | Territory of India (Art. 1(3))
  [6:0:5.0] States only – members of the federal system sharing powers with the Union | States + Union Territories + territories that may be acquired
[6:0:6] - So **UTs are covered by "Territory of India" and not by "Union of India"** (APPSC G1: **Only IV**); both expressions are in the Constitution, and the **First Schedule** lists **States and UTs**.
[6:0:7] - Grama Sachivalayam 2020: the **Union of India** includes **States only (A only)**.
[6:0:8] - **Acquiring territory:** India as a sovereign State acquires foreign territory **"as understood in public international law"** – cession, conquest, occupation (**N. Masthan Sahib v. Chief Commissioner, Pondicherry**); e.g. **Pondicherry, Goa, Daman and Diu, Dadra and Nagar Haveli, Sikkim**. Such territory need not join an existing State (it may be a UT).
[6:0:9] # First Schedule
[6:0:10] - **First Schedule = names of the States and UTs and their territorial extent** → it **must be amended to create a new State** (UPSC 2001; APPSC G2 Mains 2019; APPSC HO 2021). Fourth Schedule = Rajya Sabha seats (also amended).
[6:0:11] # Article 3 – power and procedure
[6:0:12] - **Power vests in Parliament** (not the President, the Council of Ministers or an SRC): form a new State by **separating** territory, **uniting** States or parts, uniting territory to a part of a State; **increase or diminish** area; alter **boundaries or name**.
[6:0:13] - **Admission of new States is NOT under Art. 3** – it is **Art. 2**; creating a new State out of existing States is **Art. 3** (CGPSC 2014).
[6:0:14] - **Procedure:** Bill only on the **President's recommendation**; President **refers it to the State legislature concerned** for views within a time limit; **Parliament is not bound** by the views; **no fresh reference** needed for each amendment to the Bill; for a **UT, no reference** at all.
[6:0:15] - **Consent of the State is not required** to alter its boundaries – Parliament acts "without their consent".
[6:0:16] - Passed by a **simple majority** in the ordinary legislative process (**Art. 4(2)**).
[6:0:17] - **Renaming Kerala → 'Keralam'**: Union Cabinet approval began the **Article 3** procedure.
[6:0:18] - Parliament **cannot cede** territory under Art. 3 – cession needs an **Art. 368** amendment (see Cession).
[6:0:19] ~~ Sources: NextGen Polity - APPSC Exams (full text) p.233-235, p.237 (Articles 1-4 table); Indian_Polity Part 1 (src/polity/indian-polity-part1.txt) p.47-50 (Union vs Territory, Masthan Sahib, Art. 2, Art. 3); app notes 2:6 sub 0-1
[6:0:20] - **House of introduction:** an Art. 3 Bill may be introduced in **either House** of Parliament – there is no rule that it goes first to the Rajya Sabha.
[6:0:21] - **Art. 3 Explanation I:** in clauses (a)–(e) 'State' **includes a Union territory** – an interpretive explanation, not itself a provision for forming new States.
[6:0:22] - **'State' defined (political science):** a community of persons **permanently occupying a definite territory**, **independent of external control** (sovereign) and possessing an **organised government**.
## SUB 1 | New States – Amendments and Chronology
[6:1:0] # State → year → instrument
[6:1:1] TABLE head: Year | State | Act / Amendment
  [6:1:1.0] 1953 | Andhra State | Andhra State Act – first linguistic State (1 Oct 1953)
  [6:1:1.1] 1956 | Andhra Pradesh, Kerala, Mysore… | States Reorganisation Act (1 Nov 1956)
  [6:1:1.2] 1960 | Maharashtra and Gujarat | Bombay Reorganisation Act (1 May 1960)
  [6:1:1.3] 1963 | Nagaland (1 Dec 1963) | State of Nagaland Act, 1962 + 13th Amendment (1962), Art. 371A
  [6:1:1.4] 1966 | Haryana (from Punjab) | Punjab Reorganisation Act, 1966
  [6:1:1.5] 1971 | Himachal Pradesh (UT → State) | State of Himachal Pradesh Act, 1970
  [6:1:1.6] 1972 | Manipur, Tripura, Meghalaya | North-Eastern Areas (Reorganisation) Act, 1971; Meghalaya first an autonomous State within Assam (22nd Amendment, 1969)
  [6:1:1.7] 1975 | Sikkim – 22nd State | 35th (1974) associate State; 36th (1975) full State
  [6:1:1.8] 1987 | Mizoram (20 Feb 1987) – 23rd | State of Mizoram Act, 1986 + 53rd Amendment
  [6:1:1.9] 1987 | Arunachal Pradesh (20 Feb 1987) – 24th | 55th Amendment
  [6:1:1.10] 1987 | Goa (30 May 1987) – 25th State | Goa, Daman and Diu Reorganisation Act + 56th Amendment
  [6:1:1.11] 2000 | Chhattisgarh, Uttarakhand, Jharkhand (Jharkhand 15 Nov 2000) | Reorganisation Acts, 2000
  [6:1:1.12] 2014 | Telangana (2 June 2014) – 29th State, capital Hyderabad | AP Reorganisation Act, 2014
[6:1:2] - **Chronology drills:** Maharashtra 1960 → Haryana 1966 → HP 1971 → Mizoram 1987 (**B, D, A, C**, APPSC G1 2008); Nagaland 1963 → Haryana 1966 → Sikkim 1975 → Arunachal 1987 (UPSC 2007); Nagaland → Haryana → Meghalaya 1972 → Sikkim (UPPSC 2010); AP 1953/56 → Haryana → HP → Sikkim (UPPSC 2014).
[6:1:3] - **Formed last** among AP (1956), Gujarat (1960), Karnataka (Mysore 1956) and **Punjab** (present form **1966**) → **Punjab** (CDS 2006).
[6:1:4] - **Renamings:** **Madras → Tamil Nadu** (1969), **Mysore → Karnataka** (1973) – "Mysore renamed Tamil Nadu" is the false statement.
[6:1:5] # UT entries by amendment
[6:1:6] - **Goa, Daman and Diu** made a UT → **12th Amendment, 1962**; **Pondicherry** → **14th Amendment, 1962**.
[6:1:7] # Sikkim
[6:1:8] - **35th Amendment (1974):** Sikkim an **"associate State"** – the **first and only** associate State (inserted **Art. 2A** and the **Tenth Schedule**); came into force 1 March 1975.
[6:1:9] - **36th Amendment (1975):** after a **referendum** and **abolition of the monarchy**, Sikkim became a **full State – the 22nd** (omitted Art. 2A and the Tenth Schedule); Assembly of **not less than 32 members**. Today Sikkim is simply **part of the Indian territory** (a State in the First Schedule).
[6:1:10] - **Government of Sikkim Act, 1974** set up its first elected government.
[6:1:11] # North-East facts
[6:1:12] - **Arunachal Pradesh:** capital **Itanagar**, named after **Ita Fort** ('fort of bricks'); two National Parks – **Namdapha, Mouling**.
[6:1:13] - **Tripura:** **Part C State** (1950) → **UT** (1956) → **full State** (1972).
[6:1:14] - **Nagaland:** created by the **State of Nagaland Act, 1962**, with the **13th Amendment** inserting **Art. 371A** – UPSC 2025 accepted "came into existence on the basis of a Constitutional Amendment Act" (all three pairs correct).
[6:1:15] ~~ Sources: NextGen Polity - APPSC Exams (full text) p.236-237; Indian_Polity Part 1 (src/polity/indian-polity-part1.txt) p.48-52 (evolution of States); Constitution 35th, 36th, 53rd, 55th, 56th Amendments; pmfias.com/state-description-matching-pyq-2025 and civilsdaily.com (UPSC 2025 answer "All three"); app notes 2:6 sub 4
[6:1:16] - **Lakshadweep:** the Laccadive, Minicoy and Amindivi Islands were renamed **Lakshadweep in 1973** by an Act of Parliament (the same year Mysore became Karnataka).
[6:1:17] - **Sikkim's path:** **Indian protectorate** under the India–Sikkim Treaty of **December 1950** → associate State 1974 → full State 1975.
## SUB 2 | Union Territories – Count, Administration and Legislatures
[6:2:0] # How many States and UTs
[6:2:1] - **Now (2026): 28 States and 8 Union Territories** (APPSC 2021 key; Indian_Polity Part 1 p.48).
[6:2:2] - **29 States + 7 UTs** was true only from 2014 (Telangana) to **5 August 2019**; **28 + 7** was the count from 1987 to 2014 (APPSC Draftsman 2012 key) and again from 31 Oct 2019 to 25 Jan 2020; **28 + 8** from **26 January 2020** (merger of DNH and Daman & Diu).
[6:2:3] - **8 UTs:** **Andaman and Nicobar, Chandigarh, Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu, Delhi (NCT), Jammu and Kashmir, Ladakh, Lakshadweep, Puducherry**.
[6:2:4] - **Goa is a State** (since 1987), not a UT; **Delhi is a UT** (First Schedule) with special status as the **National Capital Territory** by the **69th Amendment, 1991**.
[6:2:5] - **Smallest UT** by area: **Lakshadweep** (about 32 sq km); largest: **Ladakh**.
[6:2:6] - UTs replaced the **Part C and Part D States** under the **7th Amendment, 1956**.
[6:2:7] # Puducherry – spread over three States
[6:2:8] - **Puducherry** UT = **Puducherry and Karaikal (in Tamil Nadu)**, **Mahe (in Kerala)** and **Yanam (in Andhra Pradesh)** → spread over **3 States**; **Yanam** is the part of a UT located in AP.
[6:2:9] - **Coastal UTs (other than island UTs):** **Puducherry** and **Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu** → **two** (Lakshadweep and A&N are islands).
[6:2:10] - **Dadra and Nagar Haveli** was under **Portuguese** rule (not French) until **1954**; **Diu** is an island in the **Gulf of Khambhat**; **Daman and Diu** were separated from Goa in 1987 (Goa statehood, 56th Amendment).
[6:2:11] # Administration
[6:2:12] - **Art. 239:** the **President administers every UT through an Administrator** (LG / Chief Commissioner / Administrator).
[6:2:13] - **Art. 239(2):** the **Governor of an adjoining State** may be the administrator – the **Governor of Punjab is the Administrator of Chandigarh**; he then acts **independently of his State Council of Ministers**.
[6:2:14] - **Lakshadweep** has its own **Administrator**, not the Governor of Kerala (UPSC 2009: only the Chandigarh statement is correct).
[6:2:15] - **Art. 240:** the **President makes regulations** for **A&N Islands, Lakshadweep, Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu, Ladakh**, and for **Puducherry** only while its legislature is suspended or dissolved (the old "Goa, Daman and Diu" entry was omitted in 1987).
[6:2:16] # Legislatures and High Courts
[6:2:17] TABLE head: UT | Legislature | High Court
  [6:2:17.0] Delhi | Art. 239AA (69th, 1991) | Own High Court (the only UT with one of its own)
  [6:2:17.1] Puducherry | Art. 239A (14th, 1962) | Madras HC
  [6:2:17.2] Jammu and Kashmir | J&K Reorganisation Act, 2019 (Art. 239A applied) | Common HC of J&K and Ladakh
  [6:2:17.3] Ladakh | None | Common HC of J&K and Ladakh
  [6:2:17.4] Chandigarh | None | Punjab and Haryana HC
  [6:2:17.5] Lakshadweep | None | Kerala HC
  [6:2:17.6] A&N Islands | None | Calcutta HC
  [6:2:17.7] DNH and Daman & Diu | None | Bombay HC (also covers Goa)
[6:2:18] - **"State" in Art. 54 (election of the President)** includes **NCT of Delhi and UT of Puducherry** (Explanation added by the **70th Amendment, 1992**); the 2019 Act applied Art. 239A to J&K but did not amend Art. 54.
[6:2:19] > Bombay HC covers Maharashtra, Goa, DNH and Daman & Diu – **Lakshadweep** is the odd one out (APSLPRB SI 2019).
[6:2:20] ~~ Sources: Indian_Polity Part 1 (src/polity/indian-polity-part1.txt) p.48-52; NextGen Polity - APPSC Exams (full text) p.233-234, p.313, p.413-415 (UT administrators, Art. 239AA); Constitution Arts. 239, 239A, 239AA, 240, Explanation to Art. 54 (70th Amendment); drishtiias.com/daily-updates/daily-news-analysis/j-k-and-article-54; app notes 2:6 sub 6
[6:2:21] - **Lieutenant Governor** heads **Delhi, Andaman and Nicobar Islands, Puducherry, J&K and Ladakh**; an **Administrator** heads Chandigarh (the Punjab Governor), DNH and Daman & Diu, and Lakshadweep.
## SUB 3 | Reorganisation Commissions, Andhra State and the SRA 1956
[6:3:0] # Commissions
[6:3:1] TABLE head: Body | Year | Members | Recommended
  [6:3:1.0] Dhar Commission | June 1948 | S.K. Dhar (chairman), Panna Lall, Jagat Narain Lal | Administrative convenience, not language
  [6:3:1.1] JVP Committee (Congress) | December 1948 | Jawaharlal Nehru, Vallabhbhai Patel, Pattabhi Sitaramayya | Rejected language as the basis
  [6:3:1.2] Fazl Ali Commission (SRC) | 22 December 1953 | Fazl Ali (chairman), K.M. Panikkar, H.N. Kunzru | Broadly accepted language, rejected "one language–one State"; reported 30 Sept 1955
[6:3:2] - **Dhar Commission** was appointed by **Rajendra Prasad** (President of the Constituent Assembly); it held linguistic States a threat to national unity.
[6:3:3] - **Pattabhi Sitaramayya** was in the **JVP Committee**, **not in the SRC**.
[6:3:4] - **SRC appointment:** announced **22 December 1953** (MHA resolution dated 29 Dec 1953); suggested **Telangana** stay separate till about **1961**.
[6:3:5] - **SRC's four factors:** unity and security of India; linguistic and cultural homogeneity; financial, economic and administrative considerations; welfare of the people.
[6:3:6] - **SRC recommended:** abolish the **Part A/B/C** classification; abolish the **Rajpramukh** institution and special agreements with princely States; abolish the Centre's general control under **Art. 371** (a question that prints "Article 171" makes that item false → "Only C" is not a recommendation).
[6:3:7] # Andhra State, 1953
[6:3:8] - **Potti Sriramulu** died (**15 December 1952**) after a **56-day fast** → **Andhra State** formed **1 October 1953** from the **Telugu-speaking districts of Madras State** – the **first linguistic State**; capital **Kurnool**.
[6:3:9] - Exams also ask "Andhra Pradesh created as a language-based State in **1953**" – they mean Andhra State.
[6:3:10] # States Reorganisation Act, 1956
[6:3:11] - In force **1 November 1956**: **14 States + 6 UTs**; **Andhra State + Telangana region of Hyderabad = Andhra Pradesh** (capital **Hyderabad**); Kerala formed.
[6:3:12] - Large-scale reorganisation **on a linguistic basis = 1956**; the related amendment = **7th Amendment, 1956** (Part A/B/C/D classification ended).
[6:3:13] - Set up **five Zonal Councils** (the Indian territory divided into **five zones**).
[6:3:14] ~~ Sources: NextGen Polity - APPSC Exams (full text) p.236-237; Indian_Polity Part 1 (src/polity/indian-polity-part1.txt) p.50-52; app notes 2:6 sub 2-3; en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States_Reorganisation_Commission (citing MHA Resolution 29 Dec 1953)
## SUB 4 | Andhra Pradesh Reorganisation Act, 2014
[6:4:0] TABLE head: Particular | Fact
  [6:4:0.0] Constitutional basis | Articles 3 and 4
  [6:4:0.1] Appointed day | 2 June 2014 – Telangana formed
  [6:4:0.2] Common capital (Sec. 5) | Hyderabad for a period not exceeding 10 years; ended 2 June 2024
  [6:4:0.3] Section 8 | Governor's special responsibility for security of residents of the common capital
  [6:4:0.4] Section 46(1) | 13th Finance Commission award apportioned between the successor States
  [6:4:0.5] Section 46(2) | Central grants for the resource gap of residual AP
  [6:4:0.6] Section 46(3) | Backward-area package for Rayalaseema and north coastal AP
  [6:4:0.7] Section 85 | Krishna and Godavari River Management Boards; KRMB headquarters in AP, GRMB in Telangana
  [6:4:0.8] Section 95 | Existing admission quotas in higher, technical and medical education continue for 10 years (Art. 371D) – "equal opportunities for quality education"
  [6:4:0.9] Ninth Schedule of the Act | Companies and corporations (Sec. 53)
  [6:4:0.10] Tenth Schedule of the Act | Institutions (Sec. 75)
[6:4:1] - The Bill proposed consequential amendments to the **Representation of the People Act, 1950** (seats).
[6:4:2] - **Services and finance:** Part VIII (Secs. 76–83) services; cash and bank balances split on population ratio **58.32 : 41.68**.
[6:4:3] - **KRMB** manages **Srisailam** and other Krishna projects.
[6:4:4] - **AP journey:** Andhra State **1953** → Andhra Pradesh **1956** → bifurcation **2014** → **Amaravati made the sole capital (2026)** by amending **Sec. 5** of the 2014 Act.
[6:4:5] ~~ Sources: Andhra Pradesh Reorganisation Act, 2014 (indiacode.nic.in) Secs. 5, 8, 46, 85, 95; civilsociety-ap.blogspot.com/2015/08/notes-on-ap-reorganisation-act-2014.html (Sec. 46 text); app notes 2:6 sub 8
## SUB 5 | Jammu and Kashmir Reorganisation Act, 2019
[6:5:0] TABLE head: Particular | Fact
  [6:5:0.0] Presidential order under Art. 370 | 5 August 2019
  [6:5:0.1] Act took effect | 31 October 2019 (Sardar Patel's birth anniversary)
  [6:5:0.2] UTs created | J&K (with legislature) and Ladakh (without legislature)
  [6:5:0.3] First LG of UT of J&K | Girish Chandra Murmu (sworn in 31 Oct 2019); then Manoj Sinha (7 Aug 2020)
  [6:5:0.4] First LG of UT of Ladakh | Radha Krishna Mathur (31 Oct 2019) – there is no "Deputy Governor"
[6:5:1] - **J&K** = the **first State converted into UTs**; separate LGs for J&K and Ladakh.
[6:5:2] - **SC upheld** the abrogation of Art. 370 and the reorganisation in **December 2023** (In Re Article 370).
[6:5:3] - **Sep 2026:** MHA proposed a new **Chapter 'K' under Article 371** for Ladakh; demand to restore J&K statehood continues.
[6:5:4] ~~ Sources: J&K Reorganisation Act, 2019 (indiacode.nic.in); NextGen Polity - APPSC Exams (full text) p.306; app notes 2:6 sub 5
[6:5:5] - **Ranbir Penal Code:** J&K's own penal code (instead of the IPC) – applied in **Jammu and Kashmir**; repealed by the 2019 Act from 31 Oct 2019 (IPC, now the **Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita** from 1 July 2024).
## SUB 6 | Cession of Territory
[6:6:0] TABLE head: Instance | Year | Outcome
  [6:6:0.0] Berubari reference | 1960 | Cession of Berubari (West Bengal) to Pakistan (Nehru–Noon 1958): Art. 3 does not cover cession → Art. 368 amendment → 9th Amendment, 1960
  [6:6:0.1] SC position | 1969 | Boundary settlement ≠ cession – done by executive action, no amendment
  [6:6:0.2] India–Bangladesh LBA | 1974 (2011 protocol) | 111 Indian enclaves to Bangladesh, 51 Bangladeshi enclaves to India; 100th Amendment, 2015; amended First Schedule entries of Assam, West Bengal, Meghalaya, Tripura
  [6:6:0.3] Katchatheevu | 1974–76 | To Sri Lanka by agreements, no constitutional amendment
[6:6:1] - The Constitution lets Parliament **change names and admit territories** by ordinary law; **ceding** territory needs a constitutional amendment.
[6:6:2] ~~ Sources: NextGen Polity - APPSC Exams (full text) p.229, p.235, p.326 (Berubari); Indian_Polity Part 1 (src/polity/indian-polity-part1.txt) p.51; app notes 2:6 sub 7

=== OWN MCQs of 2:6 (83)
[f9ff967c954dd] sub0
  Unlike the 'Union of India', what does the 'Territory of India' under Article 1(3) include?
  States only | Union Territories only | *States, Union Territories and territories that may be acquired | States and the National Capital Region only
  x: The Union of India means the States only; the wider Territory of India adds UTs and territories that may be acquired.
[faf2467d60656] sub0
  Consider the following statements about the Indian Union:
1. It is not the result of an agreement among the States.
2. No State has a right to secede from it.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  1 only | 2 only | *Both 1 and 2 | Neither 1 nor 2
  x: Both are correct: India is 'an indestructible Union of destructible States', not formed by agreement, and no State may secede.
[g80838a6c4288] sub0
  Laws made under Articles 2 and 3 for forming new States amend which Schedules of the Constitution?
  *First and Fourth Schedules | First and Second Schedules | Third and Fourth Schedules | First and Seventh Schedules
  x: Under Article 4, such laws amend the First Schedule (States and territories) and the Fourth Schedule (Rajya Sabha seats).
[g195fa9a7c1ce] sub0
  Which Article of the Constitution deals with the admission or establishment of new States into the Union?
  Article 1 | *Article 2 | Article 3 | Article 4
  x: Article 2 lets Parliament admit or establish new States from outside India; Article 3 deals with forming States from existing ones.
[g846f3a99d49d] sub0
  Consider the following statements:
1. Article 2 deals with admission of territories that are not part of India.
2. Article 3 deals with alteration of areas, boundaries or names of existing States.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  1 only | 2 only | *Both 1 and 2 | Neither 1 nor 2
  x: Article 2 covers admitting or establishing new States from outside; Article 3 covers changes to existing States.
[n77ebe5220ae] sub0
  The Union Cabinet's approval to rename Kerala as 'Keralam' began the procedure under which Article?
  Article 2 | Article 368 | Article 4 | *Article 3
  x: A change in the name of an existing State is dealt with through the procedure under Article 3.
[f135972064ea7] sub0
  On whose recommendation can a Bill under Article 3 to form a new State be introduced in Parliament?
  Governor of the State concerned | Prime Minister | *President | Chief Justice of India
  x: An Article 3 Bill needs the recommendation of the President before introduction in Parliament.
[f50804bfd39cf] sub0
  Consider the following statements about the Article 3 procedure:
1. The President refers the Bill to the State legislature concerned for its views.
2. Parliament is bound by the views of the State legislature.
3. The Bill is passed by a simple majority.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  1 and 2 only | 2 and 3 only | *1 and 3 only | 1, 2 and 3
  x: Statements 1 and 3 are correct; Parliament is not bound by the State legislature's views.
[fc552a314dbaa] sub0
  According to Article 4, what is the status of a law made under Article 2 or Article 3?
  Deemed an amendment under Article 368 | *Not deemed an amendment under Article 368 | Required to be ratified by half the States | Passed by a special majority
  x: Article 4 says such laws are not amendments for the purposes of Article 368, so a simple majority suffices.
[nb6f4a69ab31] sub0
  Under Article 1, what does the 'Union of India' consist of?
  States and Union Territories | States, Union Territories and acquired territories | *The States only | Union Territories only
  x: The Union of India consists of the States alone; the wider 'Territory of India' includes States, Union Territories and territories that may be acquired.
[n3dc7cd96096] sub0
  Puducherry and Goa are examples of territories that India:
  Ceded | *Acquired | Leased | Held in trust
  x: As a sovereign State India may acquire foreign territory by cession, treaty or conquest; Puducherry and Goa are examples of acquired territories.
[f9723438903ad] sub0
  Under Article 3, to whom does the President refer a Bill on forming new States?
  The Supreme Court | *The State legislature concerned | The Inter-State Council | The Election Commission
  x: The President refers the Bill to the State legislature concerned for its views; it is then passed by simple majority.
[nc90124cb6cc] sub1
  Through which Constitutional Amendment did Goa attain statehood?
  53rd Constitutional Amendment | 55th Constitutional Amendment | 13th Constitutional Amendment | *56th Constitutional Amendment
  x: Goa is associated with the 56th Amendment; Mizoram with the 53rd, Arunachal Pradesh with the 55th and Nagaland with the 13th.
[n64c805602f8] sub1
  In which year did Nagaland attain statehood?
  1953 | *1963 | 1966 | 1975
  x: Nagaland became a State in 1963, after Andhra State (1953) and before Haryana (1966) and Sikkim (1975).
[fc518a3eeed69] sub1
  Arunachal Pradesh became a State through which Constitutional Amendment?
  53rd Amendment | *55th Amendment | 56th Amendment | 36th Amendment
  x: Arunachal Pradesh became a State in 1987 by the 55th Amendment; the 53rd was Mizoram and the 56th Goa.
[f97960e7f0c99] sub1
  By which Amendment was Meghalaya first created as an autonomous State within Assam?
  13th Amendment, 1962 | 14th Amendment, 1962 | 36th Amendment, 1975 | *22nd Amendment, 1969
  x: The 22nd Amendment (1969) made Meghalaya autonomous within Assam; it became a full State in 1972.
[fd2429b38cc18] sub1
  By which Amendment were Goa, Daman and Diu added as a Union Territory?
  *12th Amendment, 1962 | 13th Amendment, 1962 | 14th Amendment, 1962 | 56th Amendment, 1987
  x: The 12th Amendment (1962) entered Goa, Daman and Diu; the 14th added Pondicherry and the 56th made Goa a State.
[ga7d241bca4da] sub1
  Which Constitutional Amendment made Sikkim the 22nd State of the Indian Union?
  35th Amendment, 1974 | *36th Amendment, 1975 | 53rd Amendment, 1986 | 56th Amendment, 1987
  x: The 35th Amendment made Sikkim an associate State; the 36th Amendment (1975) made it the 22nd full State.
[g3aae962cca54] sub1
  Under which Act was Haryana carved out of Punjab in 1966?
  States Reorganisation Act, 1956 | *Punjab Reorganisation Act, 1966 | Bombay Reorganisation Act, 1960 | Government of Part C States Act, 1951
  x: Haryana was formed from Punjab in 1966 under the Punjab Reorganisation Act.
[g8a9e4bb173b1] sub1
  Arrange the following States in the chronological order of their formation:
1. Mizoram
2. Haryana
3. Nagaland
4. Telangana
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
  *3, 2, 1, 4 | 2, 3, 1, 4 | 3, 1, 2, 4 | 2, 1, 3, 4
  x: Nagaland (1963), Haryana (1966), Mizoram (1987) and Telangana (2014).

=== NOTES 2:7 | Citizenship – Articles 5-11, Citizenship Act 1955, Oci and Caa 2019
## SUB 0 | Acquisition – Five Modes
[7:0:0] # Modes and Sections of the Citizenship Act, 1955
[7:0:1] TABLE head: Mode | Section | Key condition
  [7:0:1.0] Birth | 3 | jus soli (modified) – see periods below
  [7:0:1.1] Descent | 4 | Born outside India, either parent an Indian citizen at birth; birth registered at an Indian consulate – jus sanguinis
  [7:0:1.2] Registration | 5 | PIO ordinarily resident 7 years; spouses of citizens; minor children of citizens
  [7:0:1.3] Naturalisation | 6 | 12 years (11 of the preceding 14 + the last 12 months); oath of allegiance; waivable for distinguished service to science, art, literature, world peace
  [7:0:1.4] Incorporation of territory | 7 | Centre notifies who become citizens when a territory joins – Goa, Pondicherry, Sikkim
[7:0:2] - **Five modes:** **Birth, Descent, Registration, Naturalisation, Incorporation of territory** (Act passed **1955**).
[7:0:3] - **Not modes:** **property or land ownership**, **depositing money in Indian banks**, **ethnic identity**; **marriage** is not a separate mode (only a registration category). **Domicile** was a condition only under Art. 5.
[7:0:4] # Citizenship by birth – three periods
[7:0:5] TABLE head: Born in India | Rule
  [7:0:5.0] 26 Jan 1950 – 30 Jun 1987 | Citizen regardless of parents' nationality (1986 Amendment)
  [7:0:5.1] 1 Jul 1987 – 2 Dec 2004 | At least one parent an Indian citizen
  [7:0:5.2] From 3 Dec 2004 | Both parents citizens, or one Indian and the other not an illegal migrant
[7:0:6] - So today a person born in India is a citizen **only if at least one parent is Indian**, subject to other conditions (APPSC ASWO key).
[7:0:7] - **Excluded** from birth: children of **foreign diplomats** and **enemy aliens**.
[7:0:8] # Residence periods – then and now
[7:0:9] - Before the 2003 Act (in force 3 Dec 2004) naturalisation needed **1 year + 4 of the preceding 7 years = 5 years** (APPSC G1 2003 key: 5 years); **now 12 years**; registration of PIO now **7 years**; CAA communities **5 years**.
[7:0:10] ~~ Sources: NextGen Polity - APPSC Exams (full text) p.240-242 (modes, pre-2004 naturalisation conditions); Indian_Polity Part 1 (src/polity/indian-polity-part1.txt) ch. Citizenship (Sections 3-7, 2003 Amendment); Citizenship Act 1955 (indiacode.nic.in) Third Schedule; app notes 2:7 sub 3
## SUB 1 | Part II – Articles 5 to 11
[7:1:0] # Constitutional framework
[7:1:1] - Citizenship = **Part II, Articles 5–11** (Part I = Arts. 1–4 Union and territory; Part III = Arts. 12–35 Fundamental Rights).
[7:1:2] - Part II only **identifies who became citizens at the commencement (26 January 1950)**; acquisition and loss afterwards are left to **Parliament (Art. 11)** → **Citizenship Act, 1955**.
[7:1:3] - **Arts. 5–8** are the Articles that define the categories of citizens at commencement (APPSC G2 Mains 2017 key "Articles 5 to 8"); Arts. 9–11 are general provisions.
[7:1:4] TABLE head: Article | Deals with
  [7:1:4.0] 5 | Citizenship at the commencement – domiciled in India + (a) born in India, or (b) either parent born in India, or (c) ordinarily resident 5 years before 1950
  [7:1:4.1] 6 | Migrants from Pakistan to India – cut-off 19 July 1948 (after it, registration after 6 months' residence)
  [7:1:4.2] 7 | Migrants to Pakistan after 1 March 1947 who returned under a resettlement permit
  [7:1:4.3] 8 | Persons of Indian origin residing outside India – register at an Indian diplomatic or consular office
  [7:1:4.4] 9 | Voluntary acquisition of foreign citizenship → not a citizen
  [7:1:4.5] 10 | Continuance of the rights of citizenship, subject to Parliament's law
  [7:1:4.6] 11 | Parliament to regulate the right of citizenship by law
[7:1:5] - **Wrong statement trap:** "Articles **4 to 10** deal with migrants to Pakistan and persons of Indian origin abroad" – false (that is **Arts. 7 and 8**; Part II is 5–11).
[7:1:6] - Art. 6 = migrants **FROM** Pakistan; Art. 7 = migrants **TO** Pakistan.
[7:1:7] # What citizenship gives
[7:1:8] - Citizen-only rights: **Arts. 15, 16, 19, 29, 30**; the **right to vote** for the Lok Sabha and State Assemblies; the **right to become a member** of Parliament and State legislatures – "citizenship means **full civil and political rights**" (CDS 2014: all three).
[7:1:9] - Citizen-only offices: **President, Vice-President, SC/HC judges, Governor, Attorney General, Advocate General**.
[7:1:10] - **President:** in India both a citizen **by birth and a naturalised** citizen are eligible; in the **USA only a natural-born** citizen.
[7:1:11] ~~ Sources: NextGen Polity - APPSC Exams (full text) p.238-240, p.244 (Articles 5-11 table); Indian_Polity Part 1 (src/polity/indian-polity-part1.txt) ch. Citizenship; app notes 2:7 sub 0
## SUB 2 | Single Citizenship – No Dual Citizenship
[7:2:0] - India: **single citizenship** and a **single domicile** for the Union and the States – allegiance only to the Union; **no separate State citizenship** (UPSC 2021: "one citizenship and one domicile" is the only correct statement).
[7:2:1] - **Source:** **Britain**; **Canada** also has single citizenship.
[7:2:2] - **USA** and **Switzerland**: **dual** – national + State/cantonal (UPSC 2005: "India and USA both have single citizenship" is false).
[7:2:3] - India does **not allow dual citizenship** with another country (Art. 9, Sec. 9): a citizen who **acquires another country's citizenship loses Indian citizenship** – APPSC A–R: both true, R explains A (key).
[7:2:4] TABLE head: Country | Citizenship
  [7:2:4.0] India, Britain, Canada | Single
  [7:2:4.1] USA, Switzerland | Dual (national + state)
[7:2:5] ~~ Sources: NextGen Polity - APPSC Exams (full text) p.243 (single citizenship, USA, Switzerland, Canada); Indian_Polity Part 1 (src/polity/indian-polity-part1.txt) ch. Citizenship; app notes 2:7 sub 2
## SUB 3 | Citizenship (Amendment) Acts and the CAA, 2019
[7:3:0] # Amendments to the 1955 Act
[7:3:1] TABLE head: Year | Change
  [7:3:1.0] 1986 | Birth from 1 July 1987 needs one parent an Indian citizen
  [7:3:1.1] 2003 | Birth rule from 3 Dec 2004 (other parent not an illegal migrant); Overseas Citizenship for PIOs of 16 specified countries (Sec. 7A); Sec. 14A (National Register)
  [7:3:1.2] 2005 | OCI widened to PIOs of all countries except Pakistan and Bangladesh
  [7:3:1.3] 2015 | PIO card merged with OCI → "OCI Cardholder"
  [7:3:1.4] 2019 (CAA) | Route for 6 communities from 3 countries; naturalisation residence cut to 5 years
[7:3:2] - **No amendment in 2008** (APPSC key); the 1986 Act took effect on 1 July 1987 – there is no "1987 Act".
[7:3:3] - Basis moved from **jus soli to jus sanguinis**.
[7:3:4] # CAA, 2019
[7:3:5] TABLE head: Stage | Date
  [7:3:5.0] Lok Sabha | 9 December 2019
  [7:3:5.1] Rajya Sabha (Parliament passed) | 11 December 2019
  [7:3:5.2] President's assent | 12 December 2019
  [7:3:5.3] Rules effective | 11 March 2024
[7:3:6] - **Purpose:** citizenship for **persecuted minorities** – **Hindu, Sikh, Buddhist, Jain, Parsi, Christian** (**six**; Muslims not covered) from **Afghanistan, Bangladesh, Pakistan** (not Sri Lanka) – A–R: both true, R explains A.
[7:3:7] - Cut-off: entered India **on or before 31 December 2014**; citizenship counts **from the date of entry**.
[7:3:8] - **Exempt:** **Sixth Schedule** tribal areas of Assam, Meghalaya, Mizoram, Tripura and **Inner Line Permit** areas.
[7:3:9] - **2026:** Citizenship (Amendment) Rules, 2026 – affidavit on any Pakistani, Afghan or Bangladeshi passport, **surrender within 15 days** of approval; **19 Aug 2026 MHA order** – applications in 8 States/UTs processed by **District Collectors**; challenge under **Art. 14** pending before a Bench under **CJI Surya Kant**.
[7:3:10] ~~ Sources: Indian_Polity Part 1 (src/polity/indian-polity-part1.txt) ch. Citizenship (2003 Amendment, OCI, CAA); nishithdesai.com dual-citizenship-for-persons-of-indian-origin-5843 and eparlib.nic.in (16 specified countries); app notes 2:7 sub 5-6
## SUB 4 | Who Regulates Citizenship – Parliament (Art. 11)
[7:4:0] TABLE head: Authority | Role
  [7:4:0.0] Parliament (Art. 11) | Alone legislates on and prescribes conditions for acquisition and termination → Citizenship Act, 1955
  [7:4:0.1] Central Government (Home Ministry) | Administers the Act; determines questions of acquisition of foreign citizenship (Sec. 9(2)); frames Citizenship Rules (2009, amended 2026) under Sec. 18; deprives under Sec. 10
  [7:4:0.2] Election Commission | Checks citizenship only for electoral rolls (Art. 324, Art. 326 adult suffrage)
[7:4:1] - **No power** to make citizenship law: **State Legislatures**, the **President**, the **Election Commission**, the Union Cabinet, the Supreme Court, the Law Commission.
[7:4:2] - "**Exclusive power of determining the issue of citizenship**" (CGPSC 2013) → **Central Government** (Sec. 9(2)); "**competent to prescribe conditions**" → **Parliament**.
[7:4:3] ~~ Sources: NextGen Polity - APPSC Exams (full text) p.239-240 (Art. 11, Citizenship Act); Citizenship Act 1955 Secs. 9(2), 18 (indiacode.nic.in); app notes 2:7 sub 1
## SUB 5 | NRI, PIO and OCI
[7:5:0] TABLE head: Term | Meaning
  [7:5:0.0] NRI | Indian citizen ordinarily residing outside India; holds an Indian passport
  [7:5:0.1] PIO | Foreign citizen who, or any of whose ancestors, was an Indian national; card merged into OCI (2015)
  [7:5:0.2] OCI | Person registered as an OCI Cardholder under Sec. 7A of the Citizenship Act, 1955
  [7:5:0.3] Commonwealth citizen | Citizen of a member country of the Commonwealth of Nations
  [7:5:0.4] Single citizenship | One citizenship for all of India
[7:5:1] - **OCI eligible:** foreign citizens of Indian origin, their **children/minor children of Indian citizens**, **foreign-origin spouses** of Indian citizens or OCIs.
[7:5:2] - **OCI barred:** if the person, a **parent or grandparent** was a citizen of **Pakistan or Bangladesh** – so an Indian who migrated to Pakistan after Partition is not eligible.
[7:5:3] - **OCI gets:** **lifelong (multiple-entry) visa**; OCI card alone enough to enter India (since 2016). **Not:** voting, contesting elections, constitutional offices; OCIs are **not citizens**.
[7:5:4] - **2015 Amendment** did **not** give NRIs any new voting right; **2026 Rules:** e-OCI cards.
[7:5:5] ~~ Sources: NextGen Polity - APPSC Exams (full text) p.243 (OCI and PIO merger); Indian_Polity Part 1 (src/polity/indian-polity-part1.txt) ch. Citizenship (OCI rights); app notes 2:7 sub 7
## SUB 6 | Loss of Citizenship – Three Modes
[7:6:0] TABLE head: Mode | Section | How
  [7:6:0.0] Renunciation | 8 | Voluntary, by declaration; minor children lose it too but may resume within 1 year of majority
  [7:6:0.1] Termination | 9 | Automatic on voluntarily acquiring foreign citizenship
  [7:6:0.2] Deprivation | 10 | Compulsory, by the Central Government; only citizens by naturalisation, registration or Art. 5(c)
[7:6:1] - **Deprivation grounds:** fraud; **disloyalty** to the Constitution; helping an enemy in war; **2 years' imprisonment within 5 years** of naturalisation/registration; **7 years' continuous residence abroad**.
[7:6:2] - **A naturalised citizen can be deprived** (Sec. 10) – "never deprived" is false.
[7:6:3] - **No effect** on citizenship: **marrying a foreigner**, **criticising the government**, bank deposits, property → loss only by **renouncing** or **acquiring foreign citizenship** (UPPSC: **1 and 2 only**); all three modes → "All of the above" (CGPSC 2013).
[7:6:4] TABLE head: Section | Subject
  [7:6:4.0] 7A | Registration of OCI cardholders
  [7:6:4.1] 14A | National Register of Indian Citizens and national identity card
  [7:6:4.2] 18 | Power to make Rules
[7:6:5] ~~ Sources: NextGen Polity - APPSC Exams (full text) p.242-243; Indian_Polity Part 1 (src/polity/indian-polity-part1.txt) ch. Citizenship; app notes 2:7 sub 4
## SUB 7 | NRC, NPR and Proof of Citizenship
[7:7:0] - **NRC (Assam):** identifies **genuine citizens in Assam**; eligibility = names in the **NRC of 1951** or electoral rolls up to **24 March 1971** (cut-off of the **Assam Accord, 1985**); **draft NRC published for Assam** (2018). The **2019 CAA was not an NRC amendment** – the Accord was given effect by **Sec. 6A** (1985).
[7:7:1] - **NRC** (citizens only) is a **subset of the NPR** (all usual residents); Sec. **14A** of the Citizenship Act.
[7:7:2] - **Not proof of citizenship:** **Aadhaar** (identity only, **Sec. 9, Aadhaar Act**), voter ID, ration card, passport.
[7:7:3] ~~ Sources: Indian_Polity Part 1 (src/polity/indian-polity-part1.txt) ch. Citizenship; nrcassam.nic.in (eligibility, 24 March 1971); app notes 2:7 sub 8
[7:7:4] - **RIIN:** **Nagaland** proposed a **Register of Indigenous Inhabitants of Nagaland** (June 2019; cut-off 1 December 1963) – a State-level register, like Assam's NRC.
